Preserve the separation between deterministic CRM state and AI-assisted development/orchestration.\n6\t\n7\t## Required workflow\n8\t\n9\t1. Read `PRODUCT.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `DECISIONS.md` and the relevant module files before changing code. Before product, positioning, roadmap or public-distribution decisions, read `docs/strategy/MASTER_PLAN.md` first; for implementation details, `ARCHITECTURE.md` and `DECISIONS.md` remain authoritative.\n10\t2. For a multi-file feature or significant refactor, create and maintain an ExecPlan under `docs/plans/` following `.agent/PLANS.md`.\n11\t3. Use public module services and named workflows. Never mutate CRM tables from API, MCP or UI code.\n12\t4. Any write operation must retain validation, audit and trace.\n13\t5. Keep MCP write tools narrow; destructive or code-generating tools must default to dry-run or require an explicit apply flag.\n14\t6. Do not add a production dependency unless it removes more complexity than it adds; record the reason in `DECISIONS.md`.\n15\t7. Run `npm run verify` before considering work complete.\n16\t8. Update `TASKS.md` and relevant docs when behavior changes.\n17\t9. **Domain-package work requires the `build-custom-domain-package` skill** (`.claude/skills/build-custom-domain-package/SKILL.md`, mirrored at `.agents/skills/build-custom-domain-package/SKILL.md`) and `docs/PACKAGE_AUTHORING.md`. A package imports only `packages/core/index.js`, reaches another package only through a declared capability, and is registered by one static import in `packages/domains/generated/index.js`. A single custom object is a module, not a package.\n18\t10. Follow `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` for every feature PR, and **use the `adversarial-review` skill for any milestone review or pre-merge review task** (`.claude/skills/adversarial-review/SKILL.md`, mirrored at `.agents/skills/adversarial-review/SKILL.md`). A milestone that skipped the review is unreviewed, not finished.\n19\t11. Respect the core budget rule (ADR-018): **new domain-specific business behavior does not go into `packages/core`** unless it is first proven to be a reusable runtime capability. A PR that adds a domain concept to core must say which runtime capability it is and why a domain package cannot own it.\n20\t12. Read `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md` for what is true in the repository today — merged milestone, the commit the public numbers were measured at, open PRs, production blockers — and update it in the same PR as a milestone merge. Do not put volatile status in `MASTER_PLAN.md`. **It carries no test count**: a count is measured into `site/claims.json` `measuredAgainst` by `node scripts/measure-suite.js --apply` and cited from there (ADR-027). Typing one into any document under `docs/` fails `npm run gtm:check`.\n21\t13. To learn what an application actually has, run `npm run crm -- app inspect --json` rather than assembling it from source and prose (`docs/APPLICATION_INSPECTION.md`). Read `valid`, then `problems[]`, then `limitations[]` — every limitation is a hard boundary on what you may claim. It reads checked-in source only: it opens no database, contacts no provider, and reports no runtime or authorization state.\n22\t14. **Compatibility Backfill Rule.** When you add or change a *horizontal* capability — one every domain could use, such as the package seam, a declared capability contract, module evolution, an evidence discipline or an agent-facing surface — record every existing domain's status against it in `docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md` in the same PR, using `aligned | partial | deferred | not_applicable | needs_extraction` with a one-line reason. Declaring the gap is required; closing it in the same PR is not. Do **not** refactor a legacy domain to close a row: extraction is sequenced work (`docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md`), not something a feature PR does on the way past.\n23\t15. A Solution Plan is a **checked file with a contract**, not prose with headings (`docs/SOLUTION_PLAN.md`). Write it, record the `app inspect` report it was written against, and run `npm run crm -- solution check <plan.json>` before writing code and again before the review — a plan bound to a composition that has since moved reports `PLAN_STALE`. A plan never carries a command: nothing in this framework executes one, and the validator refuses it.\n24\t16. **Parallel coding agents.** Each agent works in its own sibling worktree **outside** the repository, owns exactly one branch, and one final integrator reconciles the shared truth. The three rules are in *Parallel coding agents* below.\n25\t\n26\t## Parallel coding agents\n27\t\n28\tSeveral agents may work on one milestone at once. Three rules, because breaking\n29\tany of them has already cost this repository a wave of published numbers:\n30\t\n31\t1. **One worktree per agent, outside the repository.** `git worktree add\n32\t   ../<repo>-worktrees/<name>` — a sibling directory, never a path inside the\n33\t   checkout, so no agent's build output, temporary database or generated site\n34\t   lands in another agent's tree.\n35\t2. **One branch owner per worktree.** The agent that owns a branch is the only\n36\t   one that commits to it. An agent that needs another agent's change waits for\n37\t   the merge or rebases onto it; it does not reach into a worktree it does not own.\n38\t3. **One final integrator reconciles shared truth.** Every wave ends with a\n39\t   single pass over the files every branch touches — `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md`,\n40\t   `site/claims.json`, the JTBD matrix, the roadmaps — because a merge that\n41\t   resolves a conflict in a *measured record* silently discards a measurement.\n42\t   That is not hypothetical: a branch re-measured `site/claims.json`, the merge\n43\t   kept main's older block, and the ledger ran a whole wave behind the suite with\n44\t   every gate green. `npm run gtm:check` now fails on that particular drift\n45\t   (`scripts/measurement.js`), and the integrator pass is what catches the rest.\n46\t\n47\tSequenced in `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` §1.11.\n48\t\n49\t## Coding conventions\n50\t\n51\t- Node.js ESM and standard-library-first JavaScript.\n52\t- Use `// @ts-check` and JSDoc for public APIs.\n53\t- Prefer small explicit functions over hidden metaprogramming.\n54\t- Return domain objects, not raw SQLite rows with encoded JSON.\n55\t- Use cents for monetary values and ISO 4217 currency codes.\n56\t- Use ISO-8601 UTC timestamps.\n57\t- Log MCP diagnostics only to stderr; stdout is reserved for JSON-RPC.\n58\t\n59\t## Definition of done\n60\t\n61\t- behavior works end-to-end;\n62\t- tests cover happy path and policy boundary;\n63\t- trace and audit are visible;\n64\t- no direct table mutation outside module services;\n65\t- documentation is sufficient for another coding agent to continue;\n66\t- every claim in the docs, the ADR, the PR body and the JTBD matrix traces to a merged test — a capability and its limitation are stated in the same breath.\n67\t\n68\t## The DX Simplicity Gate\n69\t\n70\tBefore adding a new **agent-facing** command, tool, contract or namespace,\n71\tanswer these in the PR. Canonical rule and full rationale:\n72\t`docs/strategy/CODING_AGENT_DX_NORTH_STAR.md`.\n73\t\n74\t> **Goal-first outside, rigorous inside.** New internal complexity is justified\n75\t> only if it reduces perceived user or agent complexity, or measurably improves\n76\t> reliability or evidence.\n77\t\n78\t- name the concrete agent **failure mode** it prevents — the failure, not the capability;\n79\t- prove existing primitives are insufficient, having tried to extend one;\n80\t- minimise semantic overlap: two commands answering nearly the same question is worse than one that answers it completely;\n81\t- keep it deferred or on-demand unless every session needs it;\n82\t- preserve portability — behaviour belongs in the CLI, a JSON contract, the Package Contract, canonical Skill semantics or the Quality Gates, never in harness-specific logic;\n83\t- ship machine-readable evidence of its value: an exit code, a contract-versioned document, a fingerprint, a measured number;\n84\t- if the capability is horizontal, update the Compatibility Backfill Rule and the Legacy Alignment Matrix in the same PR;\n85\t- show the end-user goal flow gets **simpler**, not more manual. \"The agent now has one more thing to run\" fails the first bullet.\n86\t\n87\t## Code review rules\n88\t\n89\t- Flag any API/MCP handler that executes SQL directly.\n90\t- Flag any mutation without actor context and audit event.\n91\t- Flag AI-generated business decisions that are not encoded as explicit policy or approval.\n92\t- Flag money represented as floating-point currency amounts.\n93\t- Flag domain-specific business behavior added to `packages/core` without the ADR-018 justification.\n94\t- Flag a JTBD row promoted without linked evidence, or a document claiming a capability the tests do not prove.\n95\t- Flag a new agent-facing command, tool or contract that does not clear the DX Simplicity Gate {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01TGier5NYisfL7oXe8N2UtT","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Architecture\n2\t\n3\t## Core rule\n4\t\n5\t> CRM state is deterministic. AI agents interpret the user's intent and compose the system, but state changes pass through services and workflows.\n6\t\n7\t## Medusa-inspired mapping\n8\t\n9\t| Medusa concept | Accordo equivalent |\n10\t|---|---|\n11\t| Commerce module | CRM domain module |\n12\t| Product/customer/order | Offer/company/opportunity or contract |\n13\t| Workflow | Commercial process |\n14\t| Provider | Email, calendar, ERP or enrichment adapter |\n15\t| Admin extension | CRM workspace extension |\n16\t| Cloud CLI | Serve, migrate, inspect, trace and deploy commands |\n17\t| Docs MCP | Project schema, workflow and operational context |\n18\t| Agent skills | Repeatable module/workflow implementation instructions |\n19\t\n20\t## Layers\n21\t\n22\t```text\n23\tAdmin / API / SDK\n24\t        ↓\n25\tWorkflows and policies\n26\t        ↓\n27\tCRM module services\n28\t        ↓\n29\tSQLite adapter\n30\t\n31\tMCP + CLI sit beside the application and invoke the same public layer.\n32\tTrace + audit observe every controlled mutation.\n33\t```\n34\t\n35\t## Modules\n36\t\n37\tA module owns one domain boundary. It exposes a service and metadata. Other modules do not update its tables directly.\n38\t\n39\tCurrent modules:\n40\t\n41\t- Company\n42\t- Contact\n43\t- Opportunity\n44\t- Approval\n45\t\n46\t## Workflows\n47\t\n48\tA workflow is a named sequence of traced steps. It coordinates modules and providers. A step can optionally define compensation for reversible side effects.\n49\t\n50\tCurrent workflows:\n51\t\n52\t- `request-opportunity-stage-change`\n53\t- `decide-opportunity-approval`\n54\t\n55\t## Actions\n56\t\n57\tA record action is a lifecycle operation on a single record that is more than a field edit — qualify a lead, close an opportunity. Actions are code-first definitions (`packages/actions/generated/index.js`) exposed through one generic route, SDK method and Admin control rendered from metadata, never per-action generated files and never a declarative DSL.\n58\t\n59\tThe action runtime wraps `execute` in the guarantees a hand-rolled service method would not have: all business writes in one outer transaction (generated-service savepoints nest inside it), domain events held in a transaction-scoped outbox and dispatched only after commit, and a workflow trace written after the transaction resolves so a failed action still leaves a record. Fields a workflow owns are declared `writable: \"managed\"` in the manifest and refused by generic CRUD, so lifecycle state is enforced at the service boundary rather than in the UI.\n60\t\n61\tUse an action for a single-record lifecycle step; use a workflow for a multi-record or multi-step process, or when a human approval gate is involved. See `docs/ACTIONS.md`, ADR-011 and ADR-012.\n62\t\n63\t## Domain packages\n64\t\n65\tDomain behavior lives in optional packages built on the runtime's contracts, not in the core (ADR-018). A package declares itself as plain data — a name, its record manifests, its versioned policies, its actions and function-free schema metadata — and a project enables it with one static import in `packages/domains/generated/index.js`. The runtime validates the declaration, fingerprints each policy version (ADR-015), registers the actions and publishes the metadata under `/api/schema`; it never learns the domain's vocabulary. Removing the import removes the domain, and everything else keeps working.\n66\t\n67\t`packages/contracts/` (Milestone 12: contract activation and subscriptions) is the first one, and a third-party package uses the identical path. See `docs/CONTRACT_ACTIVATION.md` and the ADR-018 addendum.\n68\t\n69\t## Providers\n70\t\n71\tProviders isolate external systems from business logic. The proof of concept includes a notification provider contract and an in-memory implementation. Email, calendar, ERP and marketing providers belong here later.\n72\t\n73\t## MCP responsibilities\n74\t\n75\tMCP exposes:\n76\t\n77\t- project and CRM context as resources;\n78\t- controlled application operations as tools;\n79\t- reusable build/debug prompts.\n80\t\n81\tIt does not bypass services or workflows.\n82\t\n83\t## Extension rule\n84\t\n85\tA new business feature should normally add or change only:\n86\t\n87\t1. one module;\n88\t2. one workflow;\n89\t3. one API or MCP surface;\n90\t4. tests;\n91\t5. docs/decision record when architecture changes.\n92\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_013ti4Lnym6kpE2xp5bHPwPV","session_id":"dcabf585-5724-5af3-9508-8c01ce9770f6","uuid":"4bd4d7c6-c78e-4e2f-8835-d73462eaffd2","timestamp":"2026-08-13T23:18:47.068Z","subagent_type":"Explore","task_description":"Map existing discount/commercial code"}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01FcRunxvwRn6JZtr1cDCwYD","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t---\n2\tname: build-commercial-operations\n3\tdescription: Add or extend Commercial Operations in an Accordo project - catalog providers, products and price books, quotes with server-priced lines, immutable quote versions, and versioned discount policies with human approval. Use for catalog/quote/discount/CPQ work. Do not use for CRUD module changes (create-crm-module) or lead scoring/routing (build-lead-intelligence).\n4\trequires:\n5\t  tier: generated-project\n6\t  command: \"crm app inspect\"\n7\t  projectSurface: [\"packages/commercial/generated/index.js\", \"packages/core/src/commercial-money.js\"]\n8\t  repositorySurface: [\"ARCHITECTURE.md\", \"DECISIONS.md\", \"docs/COMMERCIAL_OPERATIONS.md\"]\n9\t  degradesTo: \"the composed commercial packages, actions, policies and providers reported by `crm app inspect --json`\"\n10\t---\n11\t\n12\t## Orient yourself first\n13\t\n14\t```bash\n15\tnpm run crm -- app inspect --json\n16\t```\n17\t\n18\tRead `valid`, then `problems[]`, then `limitations[]`, in that order. Every problem is fixed or reported before anything is built on top of it, and **every limitation is a hard boundary on what you may claim.** Then read `packages[]`, `capabilities[]`, `resources[]`, `actions[]`, `policies[]` and `providers[]`: that list is what exists. A capability absent from the report does not exist, whatever a record name, a label or a document suggests.\n19\t\n20\tIf the repository documents this skill names are absent, you are in a project built from this framework rather than in the framework itself. The inspection report is then the source of truth and those documents are optional background — do not guess at their contents, and do not assume a path exists because this skill names it.\n21\t\n22\t**Background, where they exist:** `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `DECISIONS.md` (ADR-016) and `docs/COMMERCIAL_OPERATIONS.md`. They are the deeper source for the rules below, not a prerequisite for them — the rules stand on their own.\n23\t\n24\t## Integrate a catalog provider\n25\t\n26\t1. Define it code-first: `{ name, version, label, config, async fetchCatalog(input, ctx) }` returning `{ sourceRef?, priceBooks[], products[], offers[] }`. An **offer** is the sellable package: `{ sourceKey, priceBookSourceKey, productSourceKey, name, active, externalOfferId?, components[] }`. A **component** is `{ sourceKey, label, chargeType: 'one_time'|'recurring', pricingModel: 'flat_fee'|'per_unit'|'volume'|'graduated', interval?, intervalCount?, unitAmountCents? (per_unit), flatAmountCents? (flat_fee), tiers? (volume/graduated), externalPriceId?, sourcePricingModel? }`. Tiers are ordered `{ upTo (inclusive, null on the final open-ended tier), unitAmountCents, flatAmountCents? }`. Out-of-contract output is refused (`PROVIDER_INVALID`) — never loosen the normalizer.\n27\t2. **Never flatten a provider model.** Map Stripe one_time/recurring and `tiers_mode` volume/graduated, and Zuora one-time/recurring with Volume and Tiered/Cumulative pricing, onto the four supported models and keep `sourcePricingModel` + external ids. For anything unsupported (metered usage, overage, proration, ramps, minimums, attribute-based pricing), mark the component `unsupportedModel: '<name>'` — the offer persists `quoteEligible: false` with a reason and is refused for quoting. Approximating it as a flat price is a defect.\n28\t3. Register it in `packages/commercial/generated/index.js` (static import, like actions/pipelines/intelligence).\n29\t4. The provider is called **outside** the write transaction under a bounded timeout. Never add DB writes to a provider.\n30\t5. Sync is idempotent by source key + declared source fingerprint: unchanged data must produce no writes/audits/events; changed data must create a **new** product version or **whole-offer revision** (with fresh component and tier rows), never an in-place edit. Historical and quoted evidence is immutable.\n31\t6. Test failure paths with a deterministic fixture (outage → `PROVIDER_FAILED`, hang → `PROVIDER_TIMEOUT`, bad shapes → `PROVIDER_INVALID`): no partial catalog, honest failed trace.\n32\t7. Real Stripe/Zuora/ERP adapters need human-approved credentials and are out of scope until then; do not claim full Stripe or Zuora support.\n33\t\n34\t## Build a price book and quote flow\n35\t\n36\t1. Storage modules are **read-only publicly** (every field `writable: \"managed\"` → capabilities `get`/`list`, no public create/update). Never add a public write path to a commercial record; use the trusted `createManaged`/`applyManaged` from action code.\n37\t2. Every price and total is **server-derived** from catalog data. A client may send `offerId`, `quantity` and `discountBps` — never an amount, a tier or a total.\n38\t3. Use `computeComponentAmount`/`computeLineBreakdown`/`groupComponentTotals` from `packages/core/src/commercial-money.js`. Do not hand-roll money arithmetic and never use floating-point percentages. Respect quantity semantics: **flat fees are charged once per line**, per-unit multiplies, volume prices the whole quantity at the reached tier, graduated prices each band.\n39\t4. **Never combine unlike periods.** Persist and display the one-time total plus one total per `(currency, interval, intervalCount)`. Do not derive ARR/MRR/TCV — contract term is not modeled.\n40\t5. Guard draft edits with `expectedRevision` (optimistic concurrency) and gate every action with `fromStates`. A quote binds one price book and one currency; a mismatched, inactive, incomplete or quote-ineligible offer is refused. A draft line re-prices only from its pinned offer revision.\n41\t6. Never delete records: soft-remove draft lines.\n42\t\n43\t## Create a discount policy version\n44\t\n45\t1. `{ name, version, label, config, evaluate(context) }` — `evaluate` gets a deep-frozen context (totals, `maxLineDiscountBps`, `effectiveDiscountBps`, lines, opportunity evidence, actor, frozen `config`) and must return **synchronously** `{ decision: 'auto_approve'|'approval_required'|'reject', reason?, requiredApprovalKey?, matchedRule? }`. Promises, network calls, LLM calls and DB writes are forbidden.\n46\t2. **Declare every threshold in `config`.** The fingerprint is a *declared-definition* fingerprint: closure-held values are invisible to it. A closure threshold changes silently — put it in `config`.\n47\t3. **Never edit a registered version.** Changing rules or config = a NEW version registered alongside the old (the persisted fingerprint check stops the app). Rollback = publish a new version derived from an earlier one.\n48\t4. Test both branches end to end: a low discount auto-approves with no approval record; a high discount creates exactly one approval and parks the quote in `pending_approval`.\n49\t\n50\t## Human approval boundary\n51\t\n52\tOnly `actor.type === 'user'` may approve or reject (agent actors get `403 HUMAN_APPROVAL_REQUIRED`), one decision per version, decision + quote lifecycle committed atomically. `requiredApprovalKey` is a **label** — never claim Sales-Manager or Finance role enforcement before the Production Spine (auth, tenancy, RBAC).\n53\t\n54\t## Do not implement here\n55\t\n56\tSignature, envelopes, signed artifacts, Orders, taxes, currency conversion, ISO exponent handling, metered usage, overage, proration, ramps, minimum commitments, attribute-based pricing, quote PDFs, payment, billing — Signature and Order are Milestone 11; the rest is later CPQ work. Volume and graduated tiering ARE supported (M10); usage-based rating is not.\n57\t\n58\tFinish with `npm run verify` and the starter (`node examples/starters/b2b-lead-qualification/install.mjs`).\n59\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_013ti4Lnym6kpE2xp5bHPwPV","session_id":"dcabf585-5724-5af3-9508-8c01ce9770f6","uuid":"e66c1dd7-03e3-49d2-9898-8dbd2b161cc9","timestamp":"2026-08-13T23:18:47.613Z","subagent_type":"Explore","task_description":"Map existing discount/commercial code"}

{"type":"system","subtype":"task_progress","task_id":"a55e2c90160b30c7d","tool_use_id":"toolu_013ti4Lnym6kpE2xp5bHPwPV","description":"Running grep -n -i \"discount\" /home/user/agent-crm-panel/…","subagent_type":"Explore","usage":{"total_tokens":48861,"tool_uses":26,"duration_ms":53159},"last_tool_name":"Bash","uuid":"33537c45-49c0-4c17-9f6e-319e11d0662d","session_id":"dcabf585-5724-5af3-9508-8c01ce9770f6"}
{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01NhX1CoR5j6CKFFSp2K3WEW","type":"tool_result","content":"186:## ADR-016 — Commercial Operations runs on bounded primitives, immutable versions and versioned discount policy\n190:Commercial Operations v1 (Milestone 10) — catalog, quotes, discount approval — compared three shapes: **generic generated modules plus actions only** (prices and totals would be client-writable and quoted evidence mutable — the anti-bypass and immutability requirements are structurally unreachable), a **monolithic handwritten CPQ core** (maximum control, but it duplicates the validation, savepoints, audit, events, read-only capabilities, exact queries and indexes the factory already generates, and forks the storage conventions the rest of the framework proves), and **bounded commercial primitives on the hardened M9 machinery (chosen)**. Storage is ten starter-generated **read-only all-managed modules** (ADR-015 pattern: capabilities `['get','list']`, no public create/update at all, trusted `createManaged`/`applyManaged`, exact `listWhere`/`countWhere` over manifest-declared indexes). Behavior is framework-owned code-first source: `commercial-registry.js` (catalog providers + versioned discount policies on the ADR-015 declared-definition fingerprint mechanism), `catalog-sync.js`, `commercial-money.js` and `commercial-actions.js`. **No pricing-expression language, no CPQ/rules DSL.** Company and Opportunity are referenced, never duplicated; `create-quote` is an action on the action-eligible core Opportunity module (ADR-014 precedent). Signature and Order are Milestone 11 and are deliberately absent — the immutable Quote Version is exactly the artifact they will consume.\n200:**Totals are grouped by commercial period; there is no grand total.** A quote and every quote version persist one **one-time** total plus one total per `(currency, interval, intervalCount)`. Unlike periods are never summed, and **ARR/MRR/TCV are deliberately not derived** because contract term and normalization policy are not modeled. Discount policy (v1, documented): a line's basis-point discount applies **uniformly to every component of that line, after tier/list calculation**; each component retains list, discount and net amounts, and the policy evaluates the complete grouped totals plus the component mix.\n202:**A submitted Quote Version snapshots everything needed to reproduce the commercial decision**: the offer identity and revision, product version, every component definition (charge type, pricing model, recurrence, amounts), the complete tier schedule, the calculated tier breakdown, quantity, list/discount/net per component, provider provenance, and one grouped-total row per period. A later catalog sync — including tier-boundary and price changes — leaves every existing Quote Version byte-identical, while new drafts price from the new active revision (both proven).\n204:**Money and discounts are safe-integer arithmetic with a documented rounding policy.** Amounts remain ADR-014's contract: safe integers in 1/100 currency units, two decimals, deliberately **not** ISO-4217 exponents. Currencies are uppercase `[A-Z]{3}`; a Quote binds one Price Book and one currency, and there is **no conversion** — a mismatched entry is `409 CURRENCY_MISMATCH`. Discounts are integer **basis points** 0–10000 (no floating-point percentages). Rounding, exactly: each component's list amount is computed per its pricing model with integer arithmetic only; `componentDiscount = trunc(componentList × bps ÷ 10000)` (truncating, so a discount never rounds up); `componentNet = componentList − componentDiscount` is **authoritative**; line and grouped totals are checked sums of component amounts. Every multiplication and sum is overflow-checked — a result outside the safe range is a refusal, never a silently wrong number. **The server derives every price and total from catalog data; a client cannot submit a unit price or a total through any surface.**\n206:**Quote lifecycle is server-authoritative and version history is immutable.** `draft → pending_approval → approved | rejected`, with `revise` reopening a rejected quote and `approved` terminal in M10. Draft edits (`add-line`/`update-line`/`remove-line`, soft removal — the framework has no safe general delete) are gated to `draft` by `fromStates`, validate that the offer revision is active, quote-eligible, complete, belongs to the quote's Price Book and matches its currency (a line re-prices only from its **pinned** offer revision, so a later catalog revision never silently re-prices an existing draft), and each successful edit recalculates totals and increments `draftRevision`; an optional `expectedRevision` gives optimistic concurrency (`409 STALE_REVISION`), so concurrent editors cannot lose updates. `submit` freezes an immutable **QuoteVersion** plus **QuoteVersionLines** (product, version, SKU, entry id + revision, quantity, list/net unit, discount bps, subtotal/discount/total, pricing mode/interval) whose number is DB-monotonic through a unique `qv:<quote>:<n>` key — concurrent submits produce exactly one version. Fault injection at any step rolls the whole submission back: no partial version, no orphan approval, the quote unchanged.\n208:**Discount policy is versioned, explainable and deterministic; approval is human.** A code-first `defineDiscountPolicy`-shaped definition carries `{name, version, label, config, evaluate}` on the ADR-015 mechanism: declared JSON-safe config is fingerprinted (thresholds live there, never in closures), drift under a registered version stops the boot, rollback is a new version. `evaluate` receives a deep-frozen context (quote identity, totals, max-line and effective discount bps, line summaries, opportunity evidence where readable, actor, frozen config) and must return **synchronously** one of `auto_approve | approval_required | reject` with bounded reason/approval-key/matched-rule metadata; Promises and out-of-contract results are refused. The decision, policy name, version and fingerprint are stored **on the Quote Version**, so a historical quote stays explainable after policy v2 ships. `auto_approve` approves the quote with **no fake approval record**; `approval_required` creates exactly one `quote-approval` (DB-unique per version) and parks the quote in `pending_approval`; `reject` marks the quote rejected with the policy's reason. `approve`/`reject` actions enforce the framework's human boundary — **only `actor.type === 'user'` may decide; an agent actor is refused `403 HUMAN_APPROVAL_REQUIRED`** — with one decision per version (a second is `409 ALREADY_DECIDED`), the decision and the quote lifecycle committing atomically, and concurrent decisions resolving to one winner. The core Approval module is renewal/opportunity-shaped (its table requires an opportunity FK), so quotes use a dedicated approval record rather than bending that schema; generalizing the approval domain is future work. **`requiredApprovalKey` is a label, not enforced security: real Sales-Manager/Finance role enforcement requires the Production Spine (authentication, tenancy, RBAC), and nothing here claims otherwise.**\n272:@<brand>/commercial     catalog, pricing, quotes, discount policy\n865:   ships for discount policies. Identity, version, fingerprint and declared","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_013ti4Lnym6kpE2xp5bHPwPV","session_id":"dcabf585-5724-5af3-9508-8c01ce9770f6","uuid":"f59e5bee-fbbb-4c56-98dd-a7df75fe8424","timestamp":"2026-08-13T23:19:32.600Z","subagent_type":"Explore","task_description":"Map existing discount/commercial code"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5","id":"msg_011Ce1c1RFFDwqPH2vCTNCBC","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"## Repo map for \"discount rule\" work — orientation report\n\nRoot: `/home/user/agent-crm-panel/runs/TS-01-claude-code-TS-f560c257a824-a2/fixture`\n\n**Headline finding: this already exists.** The repo has a full \"Commercial Operations\" domain (catalog → price books → offers → quotes → immutable quote versions → **versioned discount policies with human approval**), shipped as Milestone 10 and documented in ADR-016. There's a dedicated skill (`build-commercial-operations`) whose job is exactly \"add/extend a discount policy.\" Before writing anything, your teammate should assume they are *adding a new discount policy version* (or a new policy alongside it), not inventing the concept.\n\n---\n\n### 1. `AGENTS.md` — canonical guidance\n\n- Read `PRODUCT.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `DECISIONS.md` before changing code; for a multi-file feature, create an ExecPlan under `docs/plans/`.\n- **Mutation flow is strict**: \"Use public module services and named workflows. Never mutate CRM tables from API, MCP or UI code\" (rule 3). \"Any write operation must retain validation, audit and trace\" (rule 4).\n- Money must be integer cents with ISO 4217 currency codes; timestamps ISO-8601 UTC (Coding conventions section).\n- Domain-package work requires the `build-custom-domain-package` skill; a single custom object is a module, not a package (rule 9).\n- New domain-specific business behavior must NOT go into `packages/core` unless proven a reusable runtime capability (ADR-018, rule 11) — relevant since the current commercial machinery *is* in `packages/core/src`.\n- Code review rules explicitly flag: \"mutation without actor context and audit event,\" \"AI-generated business decisions that are not encoded as explicit policy or approval,\" and \"money represented as floating-point currency amounts\" — all directly relevant to a discount rule.\n- Run `npm run verify` before considering work done; update `TASKS.md` and docs when behavior changes.\n\n### 2. `PRODUCT.md` and `ARCHITECTURE.md`\n\n- **PRODUCT.md**: Accordo is a framework letting a user describe a commercial process to a coding agent, which then builds/runs/inspects/improves a CRM. Provides reusable CRM primitives, deterministic actions/workflows, provider/module extension points, trace/audit/human approval. No specific pricing/discount content here (that's ADR-016's job).\n- **ARCHITECTURE.md**: Core rule — CRM state is deterministic; state changes pass through services and workflows. Layers: Admin/API/SDK → Workflows and policies → CRM module services → SQLite adapter, with MCP/CLI beside it and trace+audit observing every controlled mutation. Domain behavior lives in optional \"domain packages\" (ADR-018) registered via one static import in `packages/domains/generated/index.js` — `packages/contracts/` is cited as the first example; Commercial Operations currently is *not yet* extracted into that package shape (see TASKS.md note below). Extension rule for a new business feature: normally touch only one module + one workflow + one API/MCP surface + tests + docs.\n\n### 3. `DECISIONS.md` — relevant ADR (grep for \"discount\", \"price\", \"quote\", \"commercial\")\n\nEssentially everything discount/quote/price-related is concentrated in one entry:\n\n**ADR-016 — \"Commercial Operations runs on bounded primitives, immutable versions and versioned discount policy\"** (line 186 in DECISIONS.md). Key excerpts:\n\n- Considered three shapes and chose \"bounded commercial primitives on the hardened M9 machinery.\" Storage is ten starter-generated **read-only, all-managed modules** (capabilities `['get','list']` only, no public create/update; trusted `createManaged`/`applyManaged`). Behavior is framework-owned code-first source: `commercial-registry.js`, `catalog-sync.js`, `commercial-money.js`, `commercial-actions.js`. **\"No pricing-expression language, no CPQ/rules DSL.\"**\n- \"Totals are grouped by commercial period... Discount policy (v1, documented): a line's basis-point discount applies **uniformly to every component of that line, after tier/list calculation**.\"\n- Money/discount rounding is explicit: `componentDiscount = trunc(componentList × bps ÷ 10000)`; discounts are integer basis points 0–10000, no floats; overflow-checked arithmetic.\n- Quote lifecycle: `draft → pending_approval → approved | rejected`, with `revise` reopening a rejected quote. `submit` freezes an immutable QuoteVersion + QuoteVersionLines/Components/Totals.\n- **\"Discount policy is versioned, explainable and deterministic; approval is human.\"** A code-first `{name, version, label, config, evaluate}` definition on the ADR-015 fingerprint mechanism; `evaluate` gets a deep-frozen context and must return **synchronously** `auto_approve | approval_required | reject`. Decision + policy name/version/fingerprint are stored on the Quote Version. `approve`/`reject` require `actor.type === 'user'` (agents get `403 HUMAN_APPROVAL_REQUIRED`), one decision per version, decision+lifecycle committed atomically. `requiredApprovalKey` is explicitly called out as \"a label, not enforced security.\"\n\nAlso referenced in **ADR-022** (line 864): \"Scoring models and routing policies use `policies`... already the shape Commercial Operations ships for discount policies,\" confirming the versioned-policy pattern is the framework's standard shape for this kind of rule.\n\n### 4. Actual commercial-operations code\n\nNo `packages/catalog`, `packages/quotes`, etc. The commercial machinery lives in two places:\n\n**a) `packages/commercial/`** — just the checked-in registry entry point:\n- `packages/commercial/generated/index.js` — exports `generatedCatalogProviders = []` and `generatedDiscountPolicies = []` (currently **empty** in this repo — a project/starter fills these in). Comment: \"Static imports only: no filesystem discovery, no dynamic loading, no eval.\"\n\n**b) `packages/core/src/`** — the framework-owned engine (per ADR-018, this is flagged in `TASKS.md` as `needs_extraction` into a real domain package, not yet done):\n- `commercial-registry.js` (235 lines) — `CommercialRegistries` class: validates/registers catalog providers and discount policies, computes declared-definition fingerprints (ADR-015 mechanism), persists/verifies them in `definition_versions` (`persistFingerprints`), and exposes function-free `metadata()` for `/api/schema`. Key exports: `validateDiscountPolicy`, `normalizePolicyResult`, `DISCOUNT_DECISIONS = ['auto_approve','approval_required','reject']`.\n- `commercial-money.js` (416 lines) — money/discount/tier arithmetic: `MAX_DISCOUNT_BPS = 10_000`, `requireBps`, `applyDiscount(listAmountCents, discountBps)`, `computeLineBreakdown`, `groupComponentTotals`, `maxLineDiscountBps(lines)`, `effectiveDiscountBps({oneTimeTotal, recurringTotals})`.\n- `commercial-actions.js` (742 lines) — the action definitions: `buildCreateQuoteAction`, `buildQuoteLineActions` (add/update/remove line), `buildSubmitQuoteAction` (evaluates the discount policy and freezes a QuoteVersion), `buildQuoteDecisionActions` (approve/reject), `buildReviseQuoteAction`, and the convenience bundle `buildCommercialActions`.\n- `catalog-sync.js` (562 lines) — idempotent catalog sync from provider output into price books/products/offers.\n\n**Where discount-policy logic itself lives, concretely:**\n- The *policy contract* (validation, evaluate signature, fingerprinting) is in `commercial-registry.js`.\n- The *policy evaluation call site* is in `commercial-actions.js`, function `buildSubmitQuoteAction` (`quote.submit` action, lines ~370–612): it builds a deep-frozen `context` (`{quote, opportunity, currency, oneTimeTotal, recurringTotals, maxLineDiscountBps, effectiveDiscountBps, lines[], components[], actor, config}`), calls `policy.evaluate(context)` synchronously (wrapped in try/catch — a thrown/async/malformed result is a hard `DISCOUNT_POLICY_INVALID` / rejected), then normalizes the result via `normalizePolicyResult`.\n- The **only concrete example of an actual discount rule/policy** is in `examples/starters/b2b-lead-qualification/commercial.js`, exports `standardSalesDiscountV1` and `standardSalesDiscountV2`:\n```js\nexport const standardSalesDiscountV1 = {\n  name: 'standard-sales-discount',\n  version: 1,\n  label: 'Standard sales discount policy',\n  config: { autoApproveMaxBps: 1000, rejectAboveBps: 5000, approvalKey: 'sales-manager' },\n  evaluate({ maxLineDiscountBps, config }) {\n    if (maxLineDiscountBps <= config.autoApproveMaxBps) return { decision: 'auto_approve', ... };\n    if (maxLineDiscountBps <= config.rejectAboveBps) return { decision: 'approval_required', requiredApprovalKey: config.approvalKey, ... };\n    return { decision: 'reject', ... };\n  },\n};\nexport const standardSalesDiscountV2 = { ...same name, version: 2, config: { autoApproveMaxBps: 500, ... }, evaluate: standardSalesDiscountV1.evaluate };\n```\nThis is the template your teammate should follow to add a new discount rule — either a new `version` of `standard-sales-discount`, or a brand-new `name`, registered via `generatedDiscountPolicies` in `packages/commercial/generated/index.js` (or a starter's own file), never by editing `standardSalesDiscountV1` in place.\n\n**Data model of a \"discount policy\" and its approval record:**\n- The policy definition itself is *code*, not a DB row: `{name, version, label, config, evaluate}`. Its identity/version/fingerprint are persisted (write-once) in the `definition_versions` table — editing a registered version's source or config stops the next boot (fingerprint drift check in `persistFingerprints`); a rollback means publishing a new version.\n- The **approval request** is a real managed record — module manifest `examples/starters/b2b-lead-qualification/quote-approval.module.json`:\n  - fields: `quoteId`, `quoteVersionId`, `sourceKey` (unique), `policy`, `policyVersion`, `policyFingerprint`, `maxLineDiscountBps`, `effectiveDiscountBps`, `requiredApprovalKey`, `status` (enum `pending|approved|rejected`), `reason`, `decisionReason`, `requestedBy`, `requestedAt`, `decidedBy`, `decidedAt`. All fields `writable: \"managed\"` (no public write path).\n- The **Quote Version** (`quote-version.module.json`, referenced from `commercial-actions.js`) stores `policy`, `policyVersion`, `policyFingerprint`, `policyDecision`, `decisionReason`, `requiredApprovalKey` alongside the frozen totals/lines — so a historical quote stays explainable even after a new policy version ships.\n\n**Automatic vs. human approval, and enforcement:**\n- `evaluate()` returns one of `auto_approve` (approves immediately, \"no fake approval record\" created), `approval_required` (creates exactly one `quote-approval` record, quote moves to `pending_approval`), or `reject` (quote marked `rejected` with the policy's reason). This happens inside `quote.submit`.\n- Enforcement of the human boundary is in `buildQuoteDecisionActions` in `commercial-actions.js` (lines ~617–661): `if (!actor || actor.type !== 'user') throw new AppError('Quote approval decisions require a human user actor', { code: 'HUMAN_APPROVAL_REQUIRED', status: 403 })`. Only a `user` actor (not an agent actor) may call `quote.approve`/`quote.reject`; one decision per version (`409 ALREADY_DECIDED` on a second attempt), decision + quote-lifecycle update committed atomically.\n- `requiredApprovalKey` is explicitly documented as a **label only** — not real RBAC — both in ADR-016 and in the skill file.\n\n**Existing tests covering discount logic** (paths only):\n- `/home/user/agent-crm-panel/runs/TS-01-claude-code-TS-f560c257a824-a2/fixture/tests/commercial-contract.test.js` — unit-level: registry validation/fingerprinting, `applyDiscount`, `computeLineBreakdown`, overflow checks, duplicate/version handling.\n- `/home/user/agent-crm-panel/runs/TS-01-claude-code-TS-f560c257a824-a2/fixture/tests/commercial-e2e.test.js` — full end-to-end: quote submit with `standard-sales-discount` v1/v2, low-discount auto-approve path, high-discount `approval_required` path with approval record, hard-reject path, revise-and-resubmit at v2 threshold, fingerprint-drift-stops-boot test, invalid `discountBps` bounds (10001, -1) rejected, concurrent-submit race.\n\n### 5. `.claude/skills/build-commercial-operations/SKILL.md`\n\nThis skill exists and directly targets \"catalog/quote/discount/CPQ work.\" Prescribed workflow for discount policies specifically (\"Create a discount policy version\" section):\n1. Shape: `{ name, version, label, config, evaluate(context) }`. `evaluate` gets a deep-frozen context (`totals, maxLineDiscountBps, effectiveDiscountBps, lines, opportunity evidence, actor, frozen config`) and must return **synchronously** `{decision, reason?, requiredApprovalKey?, matchedRule?}`. Promises, network calls, LLM calls, DB writes are forbidden.\n2. **\"Declare every threshold in `config`.\"** The fingerprint only covers declared config — a closure-held threshold changes silently and is invisible to the fingerprint check.\n3. **\"Never edit a registered version.\"** A rule/config change is a NEW version registered alongside the old; rollback = publish a new version derived from an earlier one.\n4. Test both branches end to end: low discount auto-approves with no approval record; high discount creates exactly one approval and parks the quote in `pending_approval`.\nIt also documents the human-approval boundary (`actor.type === 'user'` only, one decision per version) and an explicit \"Do not implement here\" list (signature/order, taxes, FX, metered usage/overage/proration/ramps/minimums, quote PDFs, payment, billing). It instructs orienting first via `npm run crm -- app inspect --json` and treating that report as ground truth over docs/skill text if this is a *generated project* rather than the framework repo itself.\n\n### 6. `TASKS.md` and `ROADMAP.md`\n\n- No open/in-progress item specifically named \"discount rule\" or \"new discount policy.\" Commercial Operations (M10) appears already merged and stable — `ROADMAP.md` line 20-21: \"The domain track has shipped the commercial chain end to end — lead capture through intelligence, pipeline, **quotes**, signature, order, contract activation...\"\n- `TASKS.md` line 125 (Legacy Domain Alignment Pass, not started): lists **Commercial Operations** as `needs_extraction` on the package-seam rows — i.e., there is a known, explicitly-deferred piece of work to move Commercial Operations out of `packages/core` into a proper domain package under ADR-018, sequenced *after* other milestones, and the note is explicit: \"**No domain is refactored, and nothing here authorizes starting.**\" This is relevant context but is not \"add a discount rule\" work — it's a structural extraction task, separate and currently unauthorized.\n- No other TASKS.md/ROADMAP.md line mentions a planned new discount rule/policy beyond what's already shipped (`standard-sales-discount` v1/v2 in the starter).\n\n---\n\n### Bottom line for the teammate\n\n- The discount-rule *mechanism* (versioned policy, fingerprinting, immutable quote versions, human-approval gate) is fully built and tested. Adding \"a discount rule\" almost certainly means: write a new `{name, version, label, config, evaluate}` definition (following `standardSalesDiscountV1`/`V2` in `examples/starters/b2b-lead-qualification/commercial.js` as the template), register it in `generatedDiscountPolicies` (`packages/commercial/generated/index.js` or the starter's own registration point), and add end-to-end tests for both branches (auto-approve and approval-required/reject), per the skill's checklist.\n- Do not edit an existing registered policy version in place — that's blocked by the fingerprint check and is explicitly forbidden by both ADR-016 and the skill.\n- Read the skill file in full before starting (`.claude/skills/build-commercial-operations/SKILL.md`) and run `npm run crm -- app inspect --json` first, as the skill instructs, to see current composed state before assuming what's already registered in whatever \"project\" this fixture represents."}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":152,"cache_read_input_tokens":68679,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":152,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_013ti4Lnym6kpE2xp5bHPwPV","session_id":"dcabf585-5724-5af3-9508-8c01ce9770f6","uuid":"9eb3ab68-c841-464f-b598-cc7c36d5aed6","timestamp":"2026-08-13T23:20:59.800Z","request_id":"req_011Ce1c1QQe6cRtALiLRNSkD","subagent_type":"Explore","task_description":"Map existing discount/commercial code"}

{"type":"system","subtype":"task_notification","task_id":"a55e2c90160b30c7d","tool_use_id":"toolu_013ti4Lnym6kpE2xp5bHPwPV","status":"completed","output_file":"/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-agent-crm-panel-runs-TS-01-claude-code-TS-f560c257a824-a2-fixture/dcabf585-5724-5af3-9508-8c01ce9770f6/tasks/a55e2c90160b30c7d.output","summary":"## Repo map for \"discount rule\" work — orientation report\n\nRoot: `/home/user/agent-crm-panel/runs/TS-01-claude-code-TS-f560c257a824-a2/fixture`\n\n**Headline finding: this already exists.** The repo has a full \"Commercial Operations\" domain (catalog → price books → offers → quotes → immutable quote versions → **versioned discount policies with human approval**), shipped as Milestone 10 and documented in ADR-016. There's a dedicated skill (`build-commercial-operations`) whose job is exactly \"add/extend a discount policy.\" Before writing anything, your teammate should assume they are *adding a new discount policy version* (or a new policy alongside it), not inventing the concept.\n\n---\n\n### 1. `AGENTS.md` — canonical guidance\n\n- Read `PRODUCT.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `DECISIONS.md` before changing code; for a multi-file feature, create an ExecPlan under `docs/plans/`.\n- **Mutation flow is strict**: \"Use public module services and named workflows. Never mutate CRM tables from API, MCP or UI code\" (rule 3). \"Any write operation must retain validation, audit and trace\" (rule 4).\n- Money must be integer cents with ISO 4217 currency codes; timestamps ISO-8601 UTC (Coding conventions section).\n- Domain-package work requires the `build-custom-domain-package` skill; a single custom object is a module, not a package (rule 9).\n- New domain-specific business behavior must NOT go into `packages/core` unless proven a reusable runtime capability (ADR-018, rule 11) — relevant since the current commercial machinery *is* in `packages/core/src`.\n- Code review rules explicitly flag: \"mutation without actor context and audit event,\" \"AI-generated business decisions that are not encoded as explicit policy or approval,\" and \"money represented as floating-point currency amounts\" — all directly relevant to a discount rule.\n- Run `npm run verify` before considering work done; update `TASKS.md` and docs when behavior changes.\n\n### 2. `PRODUCT.md` and `ARCHITECTURE.md`\n\n- **PRODUCT.md**: Accordo is a framework letting a user describe a commercial process to a coding agent, which then builds/runs/inspects/improves a CRM. Provides reusable CRM primitives, deterministic actions/workflows, provider/module extension points, trace/audit/human approval. No specific pricing/discount content here (that's ADR-016's job).\n- **ARCHITECTURE.md**: Core rule — CRM state is deterministic; state changes pass through services and workflows. Layers: Admin/API/SDK → Workflows and policies → CRM module services → SQLite adapter, with MCP/CLI beside it and trace+audit observing every controlled mutation. Domain behavior lives in optional \"domain packages\" (ADR-018) registered via one static import in `packages/domains/generated/index.js` — `packages/contracts/` is cited as the first example; Commercial Operations currently is *not yet* extracted into that package shape (see TASKS.md note below). Extension rule for a new business feature: normally touch only one module + one workflow + one API/MCP surface + tests + docs.\n\n### 3. `DECISIONS.md` — relevant ADR (grep for \"discount\", \"price\", \"quote\", \"commercial\")\n\nEssentially everything discount/quote/price-related is concentrated in one entry:\n\n**ADR-016 — \"Commercial Operations runs on bounded primitives, immutable versions and versioned discount policy\"** (line 186 in DECISIONS.md). Key excerpts:\n\n- Considered three shapes and chose \"bounded commercial primitives on the hardened M9 machinery.\" Storage is ten starter-generated **read-only, all-managed modules** (capabilities `['get','list']` only, no public create/update; trusted `createManaged`/`applyManaged`). Behavior is framework-owned code-first source: `commercial-registry.js`, `catalog-sync.js`, `commercial-money.js`, `commercial-actions.js`. **\"No pricing-expression language, no CPQ/rules DSL.\"**\n- \"Totals are grouped by commercial period... Discount policy (v1, documented): a line's basis-point discount applies **uniformly to every component of that line, after tier/list calculation**.\"\n- Money/discount rounding is explicit: `componentDiscount = trunc(componentList × bps ÷ 10000)`; discounts are integer basis points 0–10000, no floats; overflow-checked arithmetic.\n- Quote lifecycle: `draft → pending_approval → approved | rejected`, with `revise` reopening a rejected quote. `submit` freezes an immutable QuoteVersion + QuoteVersionLines/Components/Totals.\n- **\"Discount policy is versioned, explainable and deterministic; approval is human.\"** A code-first `{name, version, label, config, evaluate}` definition on the ADR-015 fingerprint mechanism; `evaluate` gets a deep-frozen context and must return **synchronously** `auto_approve | approval_required | reject`. Decision + policy name/version/fingerprint are stored on the Quote Version. `approve`/`reject` require `actor.type === 'user'` (agents get `403 HUMAN_APPROVAL_REQUIRED`), one decision per version, decision+lifecycle committed atomically. `requiredApprovalKey` is explicitly called out as \"a label, not enforced security.\"\n\nAlso referenced in **ADR-022** (line 864): \"Scoring models and routing policies use `policies`... already the shape Commercial Operations ships for discount policies,\" confirming the versioned-policy pattern is the framework's standard shape for this kind of rule.\n\n### 4. Actual commercial-operations code\n\nNo `packages/catalog`, `packages/quotes`, etc. The commercial machinery lives in two places:\n\n**a) `packages/commercial/`** — just the checked-in registry entry point:\n- `packages/commercial/generated/index.js` — exports `generatedCatalogProviders = []` and `generatedDiscountPolicies = []` (currently **empty** in this repo — a project/starter fills these in). Comment: \"Static imports only: no filesystem discovery, no dynamic loading, no eval.\"\n\n**b) `packages/core/src/`** — the framework-owned engine (per ADR-018, this is flagged in `TASKS.md` as `needs_extraction` into a real domain package, not yet done):\n- `commercial-registry.js` (235 lines) — `CommercialRegistries` class: validates/registers catalog providers and discount policies, computes declared-definition fingerprints (ADR-015 mechanism), persists/verifies them in `definition_versions` (`persistFingerprints`), and exposes function-free `metadata()` for `/api/schema`. Key exports: `validateDiscountPolicy`, `normalizePolicyResult`, `DISCOUNT_DECISIONS = ['auto_approve','approval_required','reject']`.\n- `commercial-money.js` (416 lines) — money/discount/tier arithmetic: `MAX_DISCOUNT_BPS = 10_000`, `requireBps`, `applyDiscount(listAmountCents, discountBps)`, `computeLineBreakdown`, `groupComponentTotals`, `maxLineDiscountBps(lines)`, `effectiveDiscountBps({oneTimeTotal, recurringTotals})`.\n- `commercial-actions.js` (742 lines) — the action definitions: `buildCreateQuoteAction`, `buildQuoteLineActions` (add/update/remove line), `buildSubmitQuoteAction` (evaluates the discount policy and freezes a QuoteVersion), `buildQuoteDecisionActions` (approve/reject), `buildReviseQuoteAction`, and the convenience bundle `buildCommercialActions`.\n- `catalog-sync.js` (562 lines) — idempotent catalog sync from provider output into price books/products/offers.\n\n**Where discount-policy logic itself lives, concretely:**\n- The *policy contract* (validation, evaluate signature, fingerprinting) is in `commercial-registry.js`.\n- The *policy evaluation call site* is in `commercial-actions.js`, function `buildSubmitQuoteAction` (`quote.submit` action, lines ~370–612): it builds a deep-frozen `context` (`{quote, opportunity, currency, oneTimeTotal, recurringTotals, maxLineDiscountBps, effectiveDiscountBps, lines[], components[], actor, config}`), calls `policy.evaluate(context)` synchronously (wrapped in try/catch — a thrown/async/malformed result is a hard `DISCOUNT_POLICY_INVALID` / rejected), then normalizes the result via `normalizePolicyResult`.\n- The **only concrete example of an actual discount rule/policy** is in `examples/starters/b2b-lead-qualification/commercial.js`, exports `standardSalesDiscountV1` and `standardSalesDiscountV2`:\n```js\nexport const standardSalesDiscountV1 = {\n  name: 'standard-sales-discount',\n  version: 1,\n  label: 'Standard sales discount policy',\n  config: { autoApproveMaxBps: 1000, rejectAboveBps: 5000, approvalKey: 'sales-manager' },\n  evaluate({ maxLineDiscountBps, config }) {\n    if (maxLineDiscountBps <= config.autoApproveMaxBps) return { decision: 'auto_approve', ... };\n    if (maxLineDiscountBps <= config.rejectAboveBps) return { decision: 'approval_required', requiredApprovalKey: config.approvalKey, ... };\n    return { decision: 'reject', ... };\n  },\n};\nexport const standardSalesDiscountV2 = { ...same name, version: 2, config: { autoApproveMaxBps: 500, ... }, evaluate: standardSalesDiscountV1.evaluate };\n```\nThis is the template your teammate should follow to add a new discount rule — either a new `version` of `standard-sales-discount`, or a brand-new `name`, registered via `generatedDiscountPolicies` in `packages/commercial/generated/index.js` (or a starter's own file), never by editing `standardSalesDiscountV1` in place.\n\n**Data model of a \"discount policy\" and its approval record:**\n- The policy definition itself is *code*, not a DB row: `{name, version, label, config, evaluate}`. Its identity/version/fingerprint are persisted (write-once) in the `definition_versions` table — editing a registered version's source or config stops the next boot (fingerprint drift check in `persistFingerprints`); a rollback means publishing a new version.\n- The **approval request** is a real managed record — module manifest `examples/starters/b2b-lead-qualification/quote-approval.module.json`:\n  - fields: `quoteId`, `quoteVersionId`, `sourceKey` (unique), `policy`, `policyVersion`, `policyFingerprint`, `maxLineDiscountBps`, `effectiveDiscountBps`, `requiredApprovalKey`, `status` (enum `pending|approved|rejected`), `reason`, `decisionReason`, `requestedBy`, `requestedAt`, `decidedBy`, `decidedAt`. All fields `writable: \"managed\"` (no public write path).\n- The **Quote Version** (`quote-version.module.json`, referenced from `commercial-actions.js`) stores `policy`, `policyVersion`, `policyFingerprint`, `policyDecision`, `decisionReason`, `requiredApprovalKey` alongside the frozen totals/lines — so a historical quote stays explainable even after a new policy version ships.\n\n**Automatic vs. human approval, and enforcement:**\n- `evaluate()` returns one of `auto_approve` (approves immediately, \"no fake approval record\" created), `approval_required` (creates exactly one `quote-approval` record, quote moves to `pending_approval`), or `reject` (quote marked `rejected` with the policy's reason). This happens inside `quote.submit`.\n- Enforcement of the human boundary is in `buildQuoteDecisionActions` in `commercial-actions.js` (lines ~617–661): `if (!actor || actor.type !== 'user') throw new AppError('Quote approval decisions require a human user actor', { code: 'HUMAN_APPROVAL_REQUIRED', status: 403 })`. Only a `user` actor (not an agent actor) may call `quote.approve`/`quote.reject`; one decision per version (`409 ALREADY_DECIDED` on a second attempt), decision + quote-lifecycle update committed atomically.\n- `requiredApprovalKey` is explicitly documented as a **label only** — not real RBAC — both in ADR-016 and in the skill file.\n\n**Existing tests covering discount logic** (paths only):\n- `/home/user/agent-crm-panel/runs/TS-01-claude-code-TS-f560c257a824-a2/fixture/tests/commercial-contract.test.js` — unit-level: registry validation/fingerprinting, `applyDiscount`, `computeLineBreakdown`, overflow checks, duplicate/version handling.\n- `/home/user/agent-crm-panel/runs/TS-01-claude-code-TS-f560c257a824-a2/fixture/tests/commercial-e2e.test.js` — full end-to-end: quote submit with `standard-sales-discount` v1/v2, low-discount auto-approve path, high-discount `approval_required` path with approval record, hard-reject path, revise-and-resubmit at v2 threshold, fingerprint-drift-stops-boot test, invalid `discountBps` bounds (10001, -1) rejected, concurrent-submit race.\n\n### 5. `.claude/skills/build-commercial-operations/SKILL.md`\n\nThis skill exists and directly targets \"catalog/quote/discount/CPQ work.\" Prescribed workflow for discount policies specifically (\"Create a discount policy version\" section):\n1. Shape: `{ name, version, label, config, evaluate(context) }`. `evaluate` gets a deep-frozen context (`totals, maxLineDiscountBps, effectiveDiscountBps, lines, opportunity evidence, actor, frozen config`) and must return **synchronously** `{decision, reason?, requiredApprovalKey?, matchedRule?}`. Promises, network calls, LLM calls, DB writes are forbidden.\n2. **\"Declare every threshold in `config`.\"** The fingerprint only covers declared config — a closure-held threshold changes silently and is invisible to the fingerprint check.\n3. **\"Never edit a registered version.\"** A rule/config change is a NEW version registered alongside the old; rollback = publish a new version derived from an earlier one.\n4. Test both branches end to end: low discount auto-approves with no approval record; high discount creates exactly one approval and parks the quote in `pending_approval`.\nIt also documents the human-approval boundary (`actor.type === 'user'` only, one decision per version) and an explicit \"Do not implement here\" list (signature/order, taxes, FX, metered usage/overage/proration/ramps/minimums, quote PDFs, payment, billing). It instructs orienting first via `npm run crm -- app inspect --json` and treating that report as ground truth over docs/skill text if this is a *generated project* rather than the framework repo itself.\n\n### 6. `TASKS.md` and `ROADMAP.md`\n\n- No open/in-progress item specifically named \"discount rule\" or \"new discount policy.\" Commercial Operations (M10) appears already merged and stable — `ROADMAP.md` line 20-21: \"The domain track has shipped the commercial chain end to end — lead capture through intelligence, pipeline, **quotes**, signature, order, contract activation...\"\n- `TASKS.md` line 125 (Legacy Domain Alignment Pass, not started): lists **Commercial Operations** as `needs_extraction` on the package-seam rows — i.e., there is a known, explicitly-deferred piece of work to move Commercial Operations out of `packages/core` into a proper domain package under ADR-018, sequenced *after* other milestones, and the note is explicit: \"**No domain is refactored, and nothing here authorizes starting.**\" This is relevant context but is not \"add a discount rule\" work — it's a structural extraction task, separate and currently unauthorized.\n- No other TASKS.md/ROADMAP.md line mentions a planned new discount rule/policy beyond what's already shipped (`standard-sales-discount` v1/v2 in the starter).\n\n---\n\n### Bottom line for the teammate\n\n- The discount-rule *mechanism* (versioned policy, fingerprinting, immutable quote versions, human-approval gate) is fully built and tested. Adding \"a discount rule\" almost certainly means: write a new `{name, version, label, config, evaluate}` definition (following `standardSalesDiscountV1`/`V2` in `examples/starters/b2b-lead-qualification/commercial.js` as the template), register it in `generatedDiscountPolicies` (`packages/commercial/generated/index.js` or the starter's own registration point), and add end-to-end tests for both branches (auto-approve and approval-required/reject), per the skill's checklist.\n- Do not edit an existing registered policy version in place — that's blocked by the fingerprint check and is explicitly forbidden by both ADR-016 and the skill.\n- Read the skill file in full before starting (`.claude/skills/build-commercial-operations/SKILL.md`) and run `npm run crm -- app inspect --json` first, as the skill instructs, to see current composed state before assuming what's already registered in whatever \"project\" this fixture represents.","usage":{"total_tokens":69075,"tool_uses":39,"duration_ms":140708},"uuid":"95b44231-3de2-42bd-a3b9-be8d88ecee13","session_id":"dcabf585-5724-5af3-9508-8c01ce9770f6"}

The short version: **this already exists** — you're not building from scratch, you're extending a shipped mechanism.\n\n## What's already here (Commercial Operations, ADR-016)\n\nThe repo ships a full chain: catalog → price books → offers → quotes → immutable quote versions → **versioned discount policies with human approval**. There's even a dedicated skill for exactly this task: `.claude/skills/build-commercial-operations/SKILL.md`.\n\n**Where the code lives:**\n- `packages/commercial/generated/index.js` — the registry entry point (`generatedCatalogProviders`, `generatedDiscountPolicies`), currently empty in this repo copy\n- `packages/core/src/commercial-registry.js` — validates/registers policies, computes fingerprints\n- `packages/core/src/commercial-money.js` — discount math (basis points, integer cents, overflow-checked)\n- `packages/core/src/commercial-actions.js` — where a policy actually gets evaluated, inside `quote.submit`\n- `examples/starters/b2b-lead-qualification/commercial.js` — the **working example** to copy: `standardSalesDiscountV1` / `V2`\n\n**What a discount policy looks like:**\n```js\n{\n  name: 'standard-sales-discount',\n  version: 1,\n  label: '...',\n  config: { autoApproveMaxBps: 1000, rejectAboveBps: 5000, approvalKey: 'sales-manager' },\n  evaluate({ maxLineDiscountBps, config }) {\n    // must be synchronous, pure — no I/O, no LLM calls, no promises\n    // returns { decision: 'auto_approve' | 'approval_required' | 'reject', ... }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n**Key rules that are already enforced in code, not just convention:**\n- Discounts are basis points (0–10,000) on integer cents — no floats.\n- `evaluate()` must be synchronous and deterministic; its result decides auto-approve / needs-a-human / reject.\n- Only a `user` actor (never an agent) can call `quote.approve`/`quote.reject` — enforced via a `403 HUMAN_APPROVAL_REQUIRED` check in `commercial-actions.js`.\n- **You never edit a registered policy version in place** — config/logic changes are fingerprinted (ADR-015 mechanism) and a mismatch stops boot. A change means registering a *new version* (see how `V2` sits alongside `V1`).\n- Every threshold must live in `config` (not a closure), because only declared config is covered by the fingerprint.\n\n**Existing tests:** `tests/commercial-contract.test.js` (unit) and `tests/commercial-e2e.test.js` (full lifecycle — auto-approve path, approval-required path, hard-reject path, revise-and-resubmit, fingerprint drift, invalid bounds, concurrent submit).\n\n**Not currently planned/in-flight:** nothing in `TASKS.md`/`ROADMAP.md` names a new discount rule. There *is* a separate, explicitly-deferred structural task to extract Commercial Operations out of `packages/core` into a proper domain package — unrelated to adding a rule, and `TASKS.md` explicitly says that's not authorized yet, so don't conflate the two.\n\n## What this means for this afternoon\n\nAdding \"a discount rule\" almost certainly means: write a new `{name, version, label, config, evaluate}` definition modeled on `standardSalesDiscountV1`, register it in `generatedDiscountPolicies`, and add e2e tests for both branches (auto-approve and approval-required/reject) — per the skill's own checklist. There's even a dedicated skill for exactly this task: `.claude/skills/build-commercial-operations/SKILL.md`.\n\n**Where the code lives:**\n- `packages/commercial/generated/index.js` — the registry entry point (`generatedCatalogProviders`, `generatedDiscountPolicies`), currently empty in this repo copy\n- `packages/core/src/commercial-registry.js` — validates/registers policies, computes fingerprints\n- `packages/core/src/commercial-money.js` — discount math (basis points, integer cents, overflow-checked)\n- `packages/core/src/commercial-actions.js` — where a policy actually gets evaluated, inside `quote.submit`\n- `examples/starters/b2b-lead-qualification/commercial.js` — the **working example** to copy: `standardSalesDiscountV1` / `V2`\n\n**What a discount policy looks like:**\n```js\n{\n  name: 'standard-sales-discount',\n  version: 1,\n  label: '...',\n  config: { autoApproveMaxBps: 1000, rejectAboveBps: 5000, approvalKey: 'sales-manager' },\n  evaluate({ maxLineDiscountBps, config }) {\n    // must be synchronous, pure — no I/O, no LLM calls, no promises\n    // returns { decision: 'auto_approve' | 'approval_required' | 'reject', ... }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n**Key rules that are already enforced in code, not just convention:**\n- Discounts are basis points (0–10,000) on integer cents — no floats.\n- `evaluate()` must be synchronous and deterministic; its result decides auto-approve / needs-a-human / reject.\n- Only a `user` actor (never an agent) can call `quote.approve`/`quote.reject` — enforced via a `403 HUMAN_APPROVAL_REQUIRED` check in `commercial-actions.js`.\n- **You never edit a registered policy version in place** — config/logic changes are fingerprinted (ADR-015 mechanism) and a mismatch stops boot. A change means registering a *new version* (see how `V2` sits alongside `V1`).\n- Every threshold must live in `config` (not a closure), because only declared config is covered by the fingerprint.\n\n**Existing tests:** `tests/commercial-contract.test.js` (unit) and `tests/commercial-e2e.test.js` (full lifecycle — auto-approve path, approval-required path, hard-reject path, revise-and-resubmit, fingerprint drift, invalid bounds, concurrent submit).\n\n**Not currently planned/in-flight:** nothing in `TASKS.md`/`ROADMAP.md` names a new discount rule. There *is* a separate, explicitly-deferred structural task to extract Commercial Operations out of `packages/core` into a proper domain package — unrelated to adding a rule, and `TASKS.md` explicitly says that's not authorized yet, so don't conflate the two.\n\n## What this means for this afternoon\n\nAdding \"a discount rule\" almost certainly means: write a new `{name, version, label, config, evaluate}` definition modeled on `standardSalesDiscountV1`, register it in `generatedDiscountPolicies`, and add e2e tests for both branches (auto-approve and approval-required/reject) — per the skill's own checklist. It should *not* mean inventing new quote/approval plumbing, that's already there.\n\nBefore touching code, the skill itself says to run `npm run crm -- app inspect --json` to see what's actually registered in this project right now (rather than trusting docs alone).
